# libGLcore: the Kona driver in the O32 and 64-bit builds

The Kona driver routines of [libglcore-driver-functions.md](libglcore-driver-functions.md) and [libglcore-functions.md](libglcore-functions.md) were read in the N32 build of the 6.5.30m `libGLcore.so`. The same routines exist in the O32 build (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so`) and the 64-bit build (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so`); the IP35 builds under `usr/gfx/arch/IP35KONA` and `usr/gfx/arch/IP35KONA64` carry byte-identical code for every function (the function code hashes of the inventory agree for all 4868 and 4907 routines), so each entry here covers both. Each build is a separate compile, so a routine's bytes differ even when it does the same thing: the O32 compiler schedules and allocates differently and keeps arguments past the fourth on the stack, and the 64-bit build has 8-byte pointers, so structure offsets in the context are different.

## Routines that match the N32 routine

Each build has two tables. The first names each routine with its address and size in this build, its address and source file in the N32 build, and the role copied from the N32 entry. The second gives what was compared for the same routine: the ordered callees, the FIFO store count and up to six of the constants. Every row is **Inferred**: the comparison was made by a script over the disassembly of each build (`objdump -d`, with a call through a register named from the global-offset-table slot loaded into it, a direct or pc-relative call named from its target address, and a call whose register was loaded before a branch target in the same routine, for example in a delay slot of another block, listed as indirect), not by reading the routine whole. For each row the script found, in this build and in N32, the same ordered list of callees (`indirect` is a call through a pointer loaded from memory, `none` a routine that calls nothing), the same number of stores to the command FIFO page at process address 0x4000, and the same set of immediate constants of 16 or more outside the process-page words 4096 to 4200. That shows the same calls, tokens and stores modulo ABI. It does not show that every branch and every context offset is the same, so the role is only as good as that match. Offsets in the N32 role are handled per build. The 64-bit build has 8-byte pointers, so its context and process-page offsets differ: each offset the role quotes is replaced by the offset found at the same place in the two aligned instruction streams (the process-page words 4112, 4120, 4136, 4140, 4144 and 4148 are at 4128, 4144, 4168, 4176, 4184 and 4192 in every aligned access of 600 or more), and a sentence whose offsets cannot be matched is dropped with everything after it. In the O32 build the layout is that of N32, so an offset is kept only when the build's code uses the same value (the process-page words are the exception: the O32 code reaches them through a register holding 4136, so they are kept as N32 has them); dispatch-table and other offsets of that kind are not carried over. A routine whose role could not be carried over is not listed here.

## Routines whose code differs from the N32 routine

The routines below are in the O32 and 64-bit builds and do not fit the tables above: their instruction streams, call order or record sizes differ from the N32 routine. Each entry says how the build differs from the N32 entry in [libglcore-driver-functions.md](libglcore-driver-functions.md). Addresses and sizes are the build's own, taken from the inventory. `scripts/build_equivalence.py` lists the routines whose callees, FIFO store count and constants match; the entries here come from reading the disassembly (`objdump -d` of the stripped library, call targets named from the global-offset slots) and from side-by-side comparisons of call order, call arguments and memory displacements. An entry marked **Established** was followed through the code it describes; **Inferred** means the call sequence, constants and offsets were compared in full but each branch was not. The entries added in the second reading round are in the same two subsections, after the first twelve. They were read from side-by-side listings of the O32 or 64-bit routine and the N32 routine (the routine's own instructions, with calls resolved from the global-offset slot or the pc-relative target); the 64-bit differences were located with the aligned instruction streams of the two builds. The thirty-one `__glkonDrawArrays*` routines other than the vertex-only, normal-only and colour, edge-flag and vertex ones were not each followed instruction by instruction: their context offsets, token constants and frames were taken from the listings by script and checked against the name's array set, and each is labelled Inferred. The 64-bit `changebasis` matrices come from `scripts/changebasis_n64_matrix.py`, which interprets the routine because Unicorn does not execute its fused multiply-add instructions.

### O32 routines read in full

- `__glkonim_SubdivPatchSGIX` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd98d2bc, 12988 bytes):
  **Established** for the setup, the command words and the vertex loop below; **Inferred** for the gather blocks. Same job as the N32 entry (the subdivision-patch command builder), organised differently in three places. First, the open note that this build "has no calls to `CopyVertex`" came from the comparison script naming a call through a global-offset page plus a small offset as the page alone: the routine calls the local `CopyVertex` four times (the call at 0xd98fad8 loads slot -32728 and adds -11724). Second, the setup loads the four array pointers from `a0` into registers, copies the eight byte-array pointers of `a1` and `a2` into stack words (sp+1092 down to sp+1064) and stores each array's count minus one at sp+1032 to sp+1044; the limits tested are 8, 9, 9 and 8, with error 0x501 on failure, as in N32. Any byte-array pointer that is null (and the whole of `a1` or `a2` when that is null) is replaced by a 32-byte zero buffer at sp+972; an eight-pass loop at +0x254 clears that buffer four bytes at a time and, guarded by index tests against 24 and 29, zeroes entries of two word tables at sp+876 and sp+760. Third, after token 0x1d1 (465) and the vertex-format word at context +0xa0fc (the code adds 0x8000 to the context and loads 8444), it branches on the format values 0x2a21, 0x2a23, 0x2a27 and 0x2a29 into four gather blocks (+0x41c, +0xbf4, +0x14b4, +0x1d98; +0x27a0 for any other value), each copying the strip vertices as 3, 4, 5 or 6 values into the local array at sp+176. The context words it writes match the N32 entry: the value count at +0xa100, the flag word at +0xa114 and the two masks at +0xa118 and +0xa11c (initially 0x00ffffff and 0x1fffffff in `s0` and `s1`). The command words follow N32: token 0x1d0 (464) with the flag word and both masks; when the flag word is not negative, the packed edge bytes (each byte read with `lb` and shifted into place, with a zero used where the matching bit of one of the masks is clear); token 0x1d4 (468) and a zero word; the vertices; a call to the end-of-primitive entry; token 0x1d2 (466) and a zero word. The vertex stage is the structural difference: N32 sends two vertices per pass, 12 passes, with the packed-vertex token in a register, while this build sends one vertex per pass, 24 passes, and re-tests the format word on every pass. That duplication is the reason N32 has 68 FIFO stores and this build 46. Per vertex the words are the token and three floats for 0x2a21; the token, three floats and a fourth value truncated to an integer for 0x2a23; the token and five floats for 0x2a27; the token, three floats, an integer-truncated fourth and two more floats for 0x2a29. The N32 routine compared is at 0xd989060, 15692 bytes.
- `__glkonResolveContext` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd96097c, 11156 bytes):
  **Established** for the structure and counts; **Inferred** for what each group sets, which follows the N32 entry's call order. The routine reads the context pointer from process word 4112 and keeps a second base, `s3`, equal to the context plus 176, so the fields of the N32 entry appear 176 lower: the packed enable words are at s3+1616 to s3+1636 (0x700 to 0x714), the five hint values at s3+1720 (0x768 to 0x778), the texture-coordinate generation block at s3+1812 (0x7c4). The dirty-group mask is at context +31584 (0x7b60): it is loaded first (bit 0x200 clears the accumulation buffer with four floats from s3+1472) and set to zero on the way out. Where N32 picks the enable or disable entry in the delay slot of one call, this build tests the bit and calls the library's enable entry (global slot -17572) in one branch or its disable entry (slot -17576) in the other with the same capability in `a0`: 133 call sites each, counted over the routine's slot loads. For example bit 0x4 of s3+1636 selects between enabling and disabling capability 0xdb2 (3506). It reloads the enable word from memory for every test, reloads `gp` after every call and leaves 471 delay slots as `nop`, which with the duplicated calls explains most of the 2540 extra bytes. The call order otherwise matches N32: the pipe-state calls from the accumulation clear to the draw-buffer selection, the current values, the depth and map-grid calls, the fog-function entry at the position where the N32 comparison shows an unnamed call, five hint calls with targets from 0xc50 (3152), the lights (vector parameters 0x1200 to 0x1202 and scalar ones from 0x1205, with 0x4000 plus the light index in `a0`) and the texture environment calls. Three groups write the FIFO directly, 50 stores in all, the same count as N32: token 0x119 (281) with a count of 12 per light, the count read from context +3588 (0xe04), then the 12 floats of each light record; token 0x1d6 (470) and a zero word, then the generation block from s3+1812; token 0x1ca (458), then `__glkon_datavn` with the word at s3+1572 (0x6d4) and a count of 61, with a zero word stored in the call's delay slot. The N32 routine compared is at 0xd961640, 8616 bytes.
- `changebasis` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd97af10, 15912 bytes):
  **Established** (the matrices below come from running this routine, not from reading its instructions). It converts a run of single-precision control values of order `n` into the coefficient set the N32 entry describes: output 0 is input 0 and output 1 is the last input, whatever the order. Run under an emulator on the unit vectors (`scripts/changebasis_o32_matrix.py`, MIPS32 big-endian, stride 1), it gives for every order from 2 to 15 a matrix whose entries are whole numbers to within 0.001. Order 3 is [1 0 0 / 0 0 1 / -1 2 -1] and order 4 is [1 0 0 0 / 0 0 0 1 / -2 3 0 -1 / 1 -3 3 -1], which agree with the two cases the N32 entry gives in words. Row 2 is (-(n-2), n-1, zeros, -1) and row 3 is (n-3, -(n-1), zeros, n-1, -(n-3)) for every order; the last row is the alternating binomial coefficients of n-1 (order 5: 1 -4 6 -4 1; the central entry of order 15 is 3432). The third row from the end in order 15 is 28, -294, 1365, -3640, 6006, -6006, 3003, 0, 0, -2002, 3003, -2184, 910, -210, 21. The arithmetic is the N32 arithmetic without fused multiply-add: 3978 instructions with 596 `mul.d`, 274 `add.d`, 277 `sub.d` and no `madd.d`, where N32 uses 236 `madd.d` and 195 `nmsub.d` in 1713 instructions; double constants are built with `lui` and `mtc1` (336 `mtc1`) rather than loaded from the constant pool (N32: 291 `ldc1`). Orders 1 to 15 are dispatched through a jump table (order minus one compared unsigned with 15, the table word added to `gp`); results go to a local array at sp+116 and are copied to the caller's output at the stride, four at a time and then singly. The N32 routine compared is at 0xd979e70, 6860 bytes.

  **Established.** Sends the descriptor's data as pairs: each pair is token 366 (0x16e) followed by the two words at the current position, for as many pairs as the count at descriptor +28; the data pointer is at descriptor +20. This build unrolls it four times: a first loop sends `count & 3` pairs, then a second loop sends four pairs per pass (eight words and four tokens per iteration, pointer advanced by 32 bytes) until the count reaches zero, so the 172 bytes send exactly the same words in the same order. - `__glkonim_PopClientAttrib` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd95e68c, 360 bytes):
  **Established.** Error 0x502 on the begin flag; error 0x504 (1284) when the stack pointer at context +12740 is not above the base at +12736. - `__glkonDrawArraysCEV` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xda68b78, 460 bytes):
  **Inferred.** Draws a run of elements from the interleaved arrays named in its suffix, in that letter order (colour base +5140, stride +5152, draw function at +35512; edge-flag base +5220, stride +5224, draw function at +35524; vertex base +5088, stride +5100, draw function at +35504). The context offsets read (5088, 5100, 5140, 5152, 5220, 5224) are exactly those of the named arrays, and the N32 routine at 0xda72e40, 440 bytes reads the same set and the same constant. Phase one sends a token word equal to 417 minus 3 times the element count in `a2`, then calls each array's draw function with that array's current pointer in `a0` and advances the pointer by its stride, for every element; phase two, when the remainder in `a3` is non-zero, sends token 386 (0x182), the remainder and two 0xdeadbeef words and runs the same element loop. The O32 routine has a 64-byte frame (N32 96) and saves 9 callee-saved registers. The loop bodies were read in full for `__glkonDrawArraysV`, `__glkonDrawArraysN` and `__glkonDrawArraysCEV`; for this routine the offsets, the constants and the frame were taken from the listing, hence Inferred.

- `__glKonInitColortableState` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd9843a0, 44 bytes):
  **Established.** The same four stores as the N32 routine at 0xd981330 (44 bytes), to the same addresses with the same values: the word at context +35640 gets context +5432, +35664 gets +5592, +35688 gets +5752 and +35712 gets +5912. The differences are in how they are written. This build forms context + 0x8000 in `v0` and stores at displacements 2872, 2896, 2920 and 2944, so the first store is the lowest address (+5432 first). N32 forms context + 0x10000 and stores at displacements -29896, -29872, -29848 and -29824, so its first store is the highest address (+5912 first). Neither build calls anything or branches; both return after the last store.

- `__glKonHostMapData1D` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd981250, 176 bytes):
  **Established.** The same loop as the N32 routine at 0xd97dd80 (204 bytes). The record at the argument is the same: word 0 selects the 12-byte entry of the context table (`ctx + 12*w0 + 35988`, read through `addiu 32767` and `lw 3221`, where N32 uses the constant 0x8c94), word 4 is the row count, word 8 the row length and word 48 the destination base. Each row calls `changebasis` with `a3 = 1`, `a2 = table + 4*row*w8` and `a0 = w48 + 4*row*w8`, the same operands as N32. O32 reaches `changebasis` through the GOT slot at gp-32732 plus -20720 on each row, where N32 keeps the callee in `s2` (slot plus -24976) and calls it with `bgezall`. O32 reloads `gp` and the row length after every call and uses a 40-byte frame; N32 uses 48 bytes with doubleword saves.

- `__glKonSendPackedVertex` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd95b080, 176 bytes):
  **Established.** The same five command words and three calls as the N32 routine at 0xd95c960 (160 bytes). Words in order: token 439 (0x1b7), the flag word `a3` unmasked, `(a3 >> 16) & 0xff`, token 463 (0x1cf) and zero. The calls are the GOT slot at gp-18404 (named `glBegin` by the target comparison), then the entry chosen from the table at gp-18632 by `(a3 >> 24) & 0xf` with the vertex arguments, then the slot at gp-18260 (named `glEnd`). O32 builds the two byte fields with `lui` then `and` then `sra` (masks 0xff0000 and 0x0f000000); N32 uses `sra` then `andi`. The values are the same. O32 reloads all three arguments of the table call from its frame (sp+32, sp+36, sp+40); N32 takes the first from `s8` and reloads the other two from sp+40 and sp+32.

- `__glkonim_BlendFunc` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xda5c67c, 192 bytes):
  **Established.** The same validation and the same command words as the N32 routine at 0xda66730 (212 bytes). The source factor is accepted for 0, 1, 0x302 to 0x308 and 0x8001 to 0x8004 (0x300 and 0x301 are rejected); the destination factor for 0, 1, 0x300 to 0x305 and 0x8001 to 0x8004. A valid pair sends token 203 (0xcb), the source and the destination as passed. A bad source sends 228 (0xe4) and 1280 (0x500) and returns without testing the destination; a bad destination sends the same two words after a valid source. O32 runs the compares as a chain of `sltiu`, `sltu` and branch tests with the constants rebuilt by `li` each time; N32 computes the same flags up front in `t0`, `a3`, `a5`, `v0`, `v1`, `a2` and `a4`. The enumerant values are from `OpenGL-Registry/xml/gl.xml` (GL_SRC_COLOR 0x300, GL_SRC_ALPHA 0x302, GL_DST_COLOR 0x306, GL_SRC_ALPHA_SATURATE 0x308, GL_CONSTANT_COLOR 0x8001, GL_ONE_MINUS_CONSTANT_ALPHA 0x8004).

- `__glkonim_ReadPixels` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA/libGLcore.so` 0xd9518b8, 208 bytes):
  **Established.** The same dispatch as the N32 routine at 0xd9531d0 (184 bytes). Begin-mode check on process word 4120 (error 0x502 through `__glSetError`). Otherwise it builds a parse record and calls `__glKonParseInput`, then `__glKonParseErrorRead` when that returns zero, then `__glkonReadPixelsOrTexels` when that returns zero too. The record layout is the same in both builds: word 0 is the width, word 4 the height, word 12 the fifth argument, word 16 the sixth, word 20 the seventh, word 24 token 0x22 (34), word 32 zero, word 36 the first argument and word 40 the second. O32 reads the fifth to seventh arguments from its caller's stack at 240, 244 and 248 and builds the record at sp+36; N32 gets them in `a4`, `a5` and `a6` and builds it at sp+0. The third call's second argument is the GOT slot at gp-32744 plus 7412 in O32 and plus 13856 in N32.

### 64-bit routines read in full

- `__glkonResolveContext` (`usr/gfx/arch/IP27KONA64/libGLcore.so` 0x2dc3a00, 8636 bytes):
  **Inferred.** The call sequence, the delay-slot choice of enable or disable, and the three FIFO groups (tokens 0x119, 0x1d6 and 0x1ca, 50 stores) are the N32 ones. The context moves.   **Established** (the matrices come from running this routine's instructions, not from reading them). The 64-bit routine gives the same result as the O32 and N32 routines: for every order from 2 to 15 the n by n matrix recovered from the unit vectors is identical to the O32 one (all 14 matrices compared entry by entry), with output 0 equal to input 0, output 1 equal to the last input, row 2 equal to (-(n-2), n-1, zeros, -1), row 3 equal to (n-3, -(n-1), zeros, n-1, -(n-3)) and the last row the alternating binomial coefficients of n-1 (central entry 3432 at order 15).
